It’s mostly because I’m not sleeping right. I’ll lie there a long time, and then if I do sleep, I wake up from bad dreams or just little noises. After that, I can’t really settle back down, so the next day I’m tired and not doing much good.\n\nDoctor: How often do the bad dreams happen? Are they almost every night, or just occasionally?\n\nPatient: Not every single night, but it’s more than just once in a while. I wake up from them pretty often, and even when it’s not a bad dream, little noises can wake me up and then I can’t get settled again.\n\nDoctor: How many nights a week would you say you have trouble sleeping like this?\n\nPatient: I’d say maybe four or five nights a week. It’s not every night, but most nights I’m either lying there a long time or I wake up from a bad dream or some little noise, and then I can’t settle back down.\n\nDoctor: Does this trouble with sleeping happen even when you’re not feeling particularly stressed or anxious, or does it seem to be related to how you’re feeling emotionally at the time?\n\nPatient: It can happen even when I don’t feel all that worked up. I’ll lie there a long time, and then I wake up from bad dreams or just little noises, and after that I can’t seem to settle back down.\n\nDoctor: When you have trouble sleeping, is it mainly that you have a hard time falling asleep at the beginning of the night, staying asleep through the night, or waking up too early and not being able to go back to sleep?\n\nPatient: It’s both, mostly. I have a hard time falling asleep at first, I’ll just lie there a long while. Then if I do get to sleep, I wake up from a bad dream or even just a little noise, and after that I can’t seem to settle back down.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever wake up feeling unrefreshed, like your sleep wasn’t restful, even if you managed to sleep for a while?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. After that, I can’t really settle back down, so the next day I’m tired and not doing much good.\n\nDoctor: How often do the bad dreams happen? It’s not every night, but most nights I’m either lying there a long time or I wake up from a bad dream or some little noise, and then I can’t settle back down.\n\nDoctor: Does this trouble with sleeping happen even when you’re not feeling particularly stressed or anxious, or does it seem to be related to how you’re feeling emotionally at the time?\n\nPatient: It can happen even when I don’t feel all that worked up. I mean, being upset probably makes it worse, but mostly I’m here because I just don’t sleep right anymore. I’ll lie there a long time, and then I wake up from bad dreams or just little noises, and after that I can’t seem to settle back down.\n\nDoctor: When you have trouble sleeping, is it mainly that you have a hard time falling asleep at the beginning of the night, staying asleep through the night, or waking up too early and not being able to go back to sleep?\n\nPatient: It’s both, mostly. Then if I do get to sleep, I wake up from a bad dream or even just a little noise, and after that I can’t seem to settle back down.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever wake up feeling unrefreshed, like your sleep wasn’t restful, even if you managed to sleep for a while?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. It’s mostly because I’m not sleeping right. I’ll lie there a long time, and then if I do sleep, I wake up from bad dreams or just little noises. Are they almost every night, or just occasionally?\n\nPatient: Not every single night, but it’s more than just once in a while. I wake up from them pretty often, and even when it’s not a bad dream, little noises can wake me up and then I can’t get settled again.\n\nDoctor: How many nights a week would you say you have trouble sleeping like this?\n\nPatient: I’d say maybe four or five nights a week. It’s not every night, but most nights I’m either lying there a long time or I wake up from a bad dream or some little noise, and then I can’t settle back down.\n\nDoctor: Does this trouble with sleeping happen even when you’re not feeling particularly stressed or anxious, or does it seem to be related to how you’re feeling emotionally at the time?\n\nPatient: It can happen even when I don’t feel all that worked up. I mean, being upset probably makes it worse, but mostly I’m here because I just don’t sleep right anymore. I’ll lie there a long time, and then I wake up from bad dreams or just little noises, and after that I can’t seem to settle back down.\n\nDoctor: When you have trouble sleeping, is it mainly that you have a hard time falling asleep at the beginning of the night, staying asleep through the night, or waking up too early and not being able to go back to sleep?\n\nPatient: It’s both, mostly. I have a hard time falling asleep at first, I’ll just lie there a long while. Then if I do get to sleep, I wake up from a bad dream or even just a little noise, and after that I can’t seem to settle back down.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever wake up feeling unrefreshed, like your sleep wasn’t restful, even if you managed to sleep for a while?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I wake up from them pretty often, and even when it’s not a bad dream, little noises can wake me up and then I can’t get settled again.\n\nDoctor: How many nights a week would you say you have trouble sleeping like this?\n\nPatient: I’d say maybe four or five nights a week. It’s not every night, but most nights I’m either lying there a long time or I wake up from a bad dream or some little noise, and then I can’t settle back down.\n\nDoctor: Does this trouble with sleeping happen even when you’re not feeling particularly stressed or anxious, or does it seem to be related to how you’re feeling emotionally at the time?\n\nPatient: It can happen even when I don’t feel all that worked up. I mean, being upset probably makes it worse, but mostly I’m here because I just don’t sleep right anymore. I’ll lie there a long time, and then I wake up from bad dreams or just little noises, and after that I can’t seem to settle back down.\n\nDoctor: When you have trouble sleeping, is it mainly that you have a hard time falling asleep at the beginning of the night, staying asleep through the night, or waking up too early and not being able to go back to sleep?\n\nPatient: It’s both, mostly. I have a hard time falling asleep at first, I’ll just lie there a long while. Then if I do get to sleep, I wake up from a bad dream or even just a little noise, and after that I can’t seem to settle back down.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever wake up feeling unrefreshed, like your sleep wasn’t restful, even if you managed to sleep for a while?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do.
